edge effect -> evidence | check the handler reply
#+end_code_graph

#+begin_note :title "The migration boundary"
The typed state-model entry points and their ordinary query path are implemented. The existing source extractors, remaining legacy consumers and default text oracle still need migration. Reusable worker pooling and language-wide totality remain open work.
#+end_note

** The SDK brings its own worker
A normal Beam Lisp program can now require the term library and ask either engine a question. The language image carries its worker and Z3 library. There is no research import path or manual worker build to arrange. The selected Source-world example works too, and its native owners retire with the call.

The language interface still carries the whole typed question. Choosing =:z3= or =:oxiz= selects an adapter; it does not change the question or hide a theory gap. This packaging slice adds no solver operations.

#+begin_rosetta
Which files are selected? | How the choice is made | What the result records
The image's defaults | Read the running language image, independently of the application's current directory. | SDK origin and actual worker/library hashes.
Explicit options | A caller names a worker or library deliberately. | Option origin, even if its bytes equal the packaged copy.
Environment pins | A configured pin applies when an option does not replace it. | Environment origin; no PATH search.
A portable release | Relocate the same image and update its actual file hashes. | Producer hashes and relocated hashes separately.
#+end_rosetta

** A question can end cleanly
Asking a formal question starts real processes. Leaving that question must close them, even when the program raises an error or the solver stops replying. The session now owns startup, its callback and cleanup as one bracket.

The new checks found two mistakes in the previous SDK. A stalled owner could survive cleanup and hide the program's original error. A reply could also contain a valid term followed by ignored bytes. Both behaviors have concrete regression checks. Cleanup now stops a stalled owner, and decoding requires exactly one complete term.

#+begin_code_graph :id formal-session-lifetime :title "The question owns its worker until it ends" :start bracket
node bracket | code | Own the whole session | implemented
summary The cleanup bracket begins immediately after startup returns. Fetching the greeting, constructing the session and running the callback all belong to it.
source research/formal-logic/sdk-src/formal/backend.bl | Session boundary | Failed initialization also closes its port and removes its borrower monitor.
node callback | code | Keep the program's original error | implemented
summary Leaving the callback retires the session and stops its owner. A deliberately suspended owner gets a one-second graceful-stop deadline, then that exact PID is killed and observed ending.
field OS lifetime | BEAM closes the owner's ports. The native worker's EOF watchdog can exit independently of its solving thread.
source test/bl/system/formal_lifetime_test.bl | Actual native owner and OS PIDs | Both real engines are checked on return, callback error, stalled owner and idle borrower death.
node wire | code | Consume the whole reply | implemented
summary Empty, oversized, compressed, malformed and trailing reply bytes are refused before the reply's identity and typed values are considered.
field Diagnostics | A bad frame, a worker exit and a hard deadline keep different failure kinds. None becomes an UNSAT answer.
source research/formal-logic/SESSION-LIFETIME.md | Framing and error interface | An incomplete packet followed by exit is reported as worker exit, matching the actual transport observation.
node fixture | code | Stop a peer that never replies | observed
summary A deliberately faulty ETF peer records complete packets and EOF. It checks handshake failure, exchange failure, request capacity and borrower death during a pending exchange.
field Scope | This peer is not a solver. Pending-peer cancellation does not qualify cancellation during a genuine hard solver search.
source test/fixtures/formal_transport_worker.py | Fault injection peer | The test uses bounded waits and removes only its own workers.
node example | code | Leave a native question with an error | observed
summary The running cell raises a callback error on each backend, checks that the original error survives, and observes both owner and OS worker ending.
source research/formal-logic/ATLAS.bl.org | Executable session-lifetime cell | The displayed statuses are produced by the actual running examples.
node boundary | constraint | Build admission before sharing workers | partial
summary The interface still owns one worker per session. A reusable pool needs bounded admission, backpressure and a reset that removes every borrower's state.
field Formal scope | These are lifetime checks. They add no termination theorem, proof certificate, global resource bound or compiler proof authority.
source research/formal-logic/INTEGRATION.md | Remaining native integration | Real busy-solver cancellation and broader transport placement remain explicit work.
edge bracket -> callback | preserves the callback's outcome
edge callback -> wire | keeps protocol failures separate
edge wire -> fixture | exercises refusal and cancellation
edge callback -> example | observes actual process retirement
edge fixture -> boundary | retains the qualification scope
edge example -> boundary | supplies execution evidence
#+end_code_graph

The selected SDK passes 686 focused assertions and 7,086 formal regression assertions. All 187 paired questions keep their exact identities and native verdicts. These execution checks do not grant compiler proof authority.

The repository check is =test/bl/system/formal_lifetime_test.bl=.

** A function can explain its failing example
Write a small function, state a promise about its output, and ask either engine about the same inputs. The function's compiler representation becomes typed terms. If an engine finds a failing input, Beam Lisp runs that exact representation and checks that the failure really happens.

This connects formal questions to a familiar programming task: /show me where this function breaks its promise/. Arguments, returned values and crashes travel as data. Return types are inferred, and an unused output cannot hide a crash.

#+begin_rosetta :title "What a source-property answer means"
Situation | Answer | What you can conclude
Native UNSAT | solver-claim | The engine reports no counterexample. Its certificate is still unchecked.
A failing input reproduces | refuted | Both the term model and execution of the translated ANF agree on the failure.
The input model is empty | vacuous | There are no admitted inputs on which to check the promise.
The source has no reviewed translation | unsupported | Sampling runs only when explicitly requested.
A portable certificate checks | checked term claim | The mathematical question has checked evidence. Its connection to compilation and runtime remains reviewed.
#+end_rosetta

#+begin_code_graph :id formal-source-properties :title "Follow a promise from source to a real failure" :start source
node source | code | Compile the function once | implemented

source research/formal-logic/sdk-src/formal/source.bl | Canonical SDK source adapter | Integer signs, nil, truthiness, lazy branches and raisedness stay explicit.

** Generators can speak the same logic
A generator can describe both concrete test inputs and a typed logical domain. The quantifier asks one complete question, whether its input is an integer, an enum with payloads, or a product containing an enum and a vector-length model. Changing the backend keeps the question's identity.

This makes a practical language feature possible: start with the generator you already use for tests, ask a solver about its reviewed meaning, then run proposed counterexamples through the real predicate. The answer says how far that process got.

#+begin_rosetta :title "An ordinary BL question, with explicit meaning"
Input | What becomes terms | What remains explicit
Enum | Full keyword identities and typed scalar payloads | Finite tags encode the alternatives; this is not a native recursive datatype.
Product | Ordered coordinates with their own value models | Mixed enum and length models compose in one question.
Vector length | count, empty?, conj, rest and pop | Elements are erased. rest returns a list; empty pop raises.
Text prefix | String/starts_with? and explicit code-point length | BL count uses graphemes. Runtime floats need their own numeric profile.
Function clauses | Actual compiled selection guards | Bodies are removed before compilation, including their macros.
#+end_rosetta

#+begin_code_graph :id formal-veritas-terms :title "From a test domain to an inspectable answer" :start domain
node domain | code | Give the generator a logical meaning | implemented

field Scope | A collection-length model erases elements. Solver text does not silently replace runtime grapheme bounds.
source priv/lib/veritas.bl | Canonical Veritas interface | Options select backend and execution engine independently.
node source | code | Translate actual compiler terms | implemented
summary Predicates use reviewed ANF intrinsics. Coverage rebuilds the original parameters and guards with selection-only bodies before compiling them.
field Selection | Clause-local names, false guards and raised guards retain their meaning. Unsupported patterns stay explicit.
source priv/lib/formal/coverage.bl | Compiler-backed clause selection | Body macros are not expanded by coverage.
source priv/lib/formal/runtime-values.bl | Composable runtime value models | Length, kind, payload identity and partiality are separate facts.
node answer | code | Distinguish a claim from a witness | observed
summary Native UNSAT remains a solver-claim with an unchecked certificate. SAT needs independent term replay, domain admission and execution of the same predicate ANF before becoming a runtime witness.
field Capability | On the same Unicode-prefix law, Z3 reports a solver claim and OxiZ reports unknown. Unknown does not trigger sampling.
source research/formal-logic/veritas-terms-check.bl | SDK-only comparison program | Both engines receive identical complete questions.

summary The ledger retains complete questions, their checked identities, caller evidence and witness presence. False and nil survive durable storage; unfamiliar modalities remain visible in reports.
field Authority | Every row has recorded assurance. Storing a label does not check a proof or grant compiler authority.
source priv/lib/veritas/ledger.bl | Durable caller records | Question identity is checked before writing.
edge domain -> source | binds semantic inputs
edge source -> answer | asks one complete question
edge answer -> ledger | preserves scope and evidence
edge ledger -> domain | makes a later comparison inspectable
#+end_code_graph

#+begin_honest :title "An abstraction is a promise with a boundary"
A length model can reason about pop without claiming to know the elements. A large proposed length remains a candidate when concrete replay cannot materialize it; the logical domain is not silently reduced. Finite exhaustive search, sampling, native claims and checked certificates keep different labels. These interfaces remain reviewed implementations, with no compiler proof authority.
#+end_honest

** A contract can generate its own responses
A response contract names its fields and keeps every law. The same relation can ask for a valid response, an isolated fault, a response breaking every law, or a global integer boundary. Z3 and OxiZ receive complete typed questions.

Two verified baselines explain why this needs care: the preceding SDK fails twelve response-boundary assertions and five semantic-assumption assertions. The replacement refuses untranslated laws, keeps vacuity separate from implication, and measures concrete violations independently. The checkpoint 37 SDK passed 1,274 focused assertions across 24 files and 8,102 regression assertions across 31 files, including all seventeen baseline assertions. The 187 preceding paired questions keep their exact identities and native verdicts. A fresh process also reopened the compound optimization evidence successfully.

#+begin_rosetta :title "The distinctions this interface must preserve"
Question | What is being asked | What an unresolved answer must retain
Valid response | Every law holds | The whole question; no successful empty answer
Isolated fault | One law fails while the others hold | Whether those other laws are feasible
Every-law fault | Every law fails | This is stronger than failing their conjunction
Integer boundary | A feasible response exists and no better response exists | Separate feasibility and optimality evidence
Fault coverage | Which supplied concrete responses violate each law | Unknown measurements create no violation edges
#+end_rosetta

#+begin_code_graph :id formal-response-contracts :title "One response relation, several questions" :start relation

source priv/lib/veritas/fault.bl | Fault sets as data | Implication claims and infeasible premises remain separate.

source priv/lib/formal/integer-optimize.bl | Backend-independent boundary search | Feasible-response evidence and the unchecked optimality claim are separate.

edge relation -> conditions | retains its declared domain

edge reply -> replay | admits the concrete point only after agreement

#+end_code_graph

#+begin_sequence :title "How a response earns its witness"
lanes Program · named fields and laws | Question · typed BL data | Engine · Z3 or OxiZ | Replay · independent checks
Program -> Question : Keep every law and every domain condition
Question -> Engine : Send the same complete question to either backend
Engine --> Replay : Return a proposed model with its native status
Replay -> Replay : Check the terms, concrete domain and exact source ANF
Replay => Program : Publish a witnessed response only when those checks agree
note Program, Replay : An UNSAT answer remains an unchecked solver claim. A missing value remains a candidate.
#+end_sequence

A native model can also be incomplete: Z3 materializes a wholly unconstrained integer, while OxiZ can leave that coordinate opaque. Its SAT answer remains a candidate. Explicit pins make the same crashing law replayable on both; missing values are never invented.

#+begin_honest :title "What this still leaves open"
The native absence and optimality claims have unchecked certificates. The source adapter and compiler connection remain reviewed implementations. Runtime-only predicates still need the callable ownership work described earlier. Integer search has a finite query budget; it does not prove unboundedness or whole-language totality.
#+end_honest

** A reply belongs to one exact question
A valid answer can become misleading when it is attached to the wrong request. Asking for zero and asking for a value above ten are different questions, even when both requests share the rule that the value is nonnegative. Every native answer now carries the complete question it actually answered, including retained assumptions and context.

The response boundary checks two things separately. First, the reply must belong to this exact question. Then the actual value being published must satisfy every condition in that question. A decoded zero cannot borrow credibility from an assignment saying eleven. A foreign UNSAT answer cannot turn a realizable request into an absence claim.

#+begin_rosetta :title "What earns a published response"
Incoming data | Required check | Result when it fails
Model from another request | Complete question identity | Interface error, no witness
Absence answer from another request | Complete question identity | Interface error, no absence claim
Decoded response contradicts its assignments | Replay actual fields against every condition | Candidate, no published value
Actual response agrees with the complete question | Native replay, concrete pins, source replay and domain admission | Witness with separate evidence
#+end_rosetta

Try [[#cell-story-answer-binding][the same four cases on both engines]]. The preceding SDK fails nine boundary assertions. The checkpoint 38 SDK passed 1,307 focused assertions across 26 files and 8,104 regression assertions across 31 files, including the repaired boundary and complete large-payload reopen. All 187 preceding paired questions keep their identities and verdicts. Native absence certificates remain unchecked.

Complete evidence can be larger than a database index key. The ledger now stores exact structured payloads in its existing content-addressed file tier and indexes a small descriptor. Reads restore the ordinary BL values and support older inline rows. Missing or corrupt payloads raise an error; the explicit payload capacity is 16 MiB. Recording a claim still grants no proof authority.

Read [[file:ANSWER-BINDING.md][the interface]], [[file:answer-binding-check.bl][the SDK-only program]], [[file:evidence/answer-binding/baseline-checks.txt][the nine preceding failures]], [[file:evidence/answer-binding/candidate-1/ledger-write-checks.txt][the retained index-size failure]], [[file:sdk-src/formal/logic.bl][the complete native answer]], [[file:sdk-src/formal/response-runtime.bl][the published-point replay]] and [[file:sdk-src/veritas/ledger.bl][content-addressed ledger storage]]. Inspect [[file:evidence/answer-binding/examples.json][the SDK-only native results]], [[file:evidence/answer-binding/ledger-read.json][the fresh-process reopen]], [[file:evidence/answer-binding/focused-checks.txt][the focused checks]], [[file:evidence/answer-binding/regression-checks.txt][the formal regression]] and [[file:evidence/answer-binding/qualification.json][the exact qualification]].

** A better question shape can help both engines
Beam Lisp now keeps two explicit questions: the one the program asked and the form an engine executes. An untrusted normalizer proposes a useful shape. A separate checker verifies equality objects for every changed assertion, claim and observation. Labels, assumptions, scope, profiles and context stay attached.

This has a concrete benefit. A factored size bound such as =(* 12 (+ 1 s0 s1))= can leave OxiZ uncertain. The distributed sum =(+ 12 (* 12 s0) (* 12 s1))= lets the same engine settle these examples. The language keeps the connection between those forms available as data.

#+begin_rosetta :title "The actual paired SDK-only comparison"
Declared size question | Z3, original | OxiZ 0.3.4, original | Both, checked affine plan
Single placement, arity two | Proved, native certificate unchecked | Unknown | Proved, native certificate unchecked
Double placement, arity two | Refuted with replay | Unknown | Refuted with valid original replay
Question connection | Same original question on both engines | Same original question on both engines | Every actual site checked independently
#+end_rosetta

Checkpoint 39 qualifies this interface on one immutable SDK: 299 new focused assertions across five files, 1,307 preceding focused assertions across 26 files, and 8,106 regression assertions across 31 files. All 187 preceding paired questions retain their complete identities and native verdicts. A separate process also reconstructs four complete ledger records and checks their connections without invoking the managed query method in that rechecking window.

#+begin_honest :title "A checked change of form has a precise scope"
Each accepted plan has checked evidence for these exact question forms. This does not prove a general normalizer correct. Native absence certificates stay unchecked. Preparation currently admits a narrow fragment, and execution owns a fresh session. Larger observation structures and transformation of borrowed live contexts remain ahead.
#+end_honest

** What a size result actually means
A constructor combines its children and adds some local overhead. The model declares a bound on that overhead and on every child's emitted size. Beam Lisp can now construct a positive arithmetic certificate, check it afresh outside the producer, and construct counterexamples as ordinary typed bindings.

When the overhead bound is eight and the coefficient is twelve, the declared single-placement model has checked positive evidence. Raising the overhead bound to thirteen gives a checked counterexample. A tiny theorem-search budget leaves the answer unknown, even when a separate native call reports proved.

#+begin_rosetta :title "Truth and evidence stay distinct"
Status | holds field | What establishes it
checked | true | Fresh positive certificate and feasible premises
refuted | false | A checked counterexample to the single-placement bound
unknown | nil | Search did not establish truth
unsupported or error | nil | An explicit interface, fragment or execution limit
Duplicated-child negative control | Separate status | Its own checked counterexample; it does not determine the single-placement result
#+end_rosetta

The theorem search and preparation search have independent budgets. The selected SDK passes all seven assertions in the retained [[file:evidence/query-preparation/verdict-baseline-checks.txt][previous candidate's six-failure review]]. The interface also rejects an empty arity family. Arity zero has a separate meaningful question: no child exists to duplicate.

Try [[#cell-story-size-evidence][the three truth outcomes]]. Read [[file:COMPILER-LINEARITY.md][the model and migration scope]] and [[file:evidence/compiler-linearity/baseline-checks.txt][the preceding eight legacy-interface failures]], now passing on the selected SDK.

#+begin_honest :title "The declared recurrence is the proved subject"
Bounded overhead, child placement, the chosen recursion measure and the connection to actual compiler emission remain explicit premises. The existing structural and emitted-artifact checks also passed; they have their own coverage. These facts do not establish whole-compiler complexity, preservation or whole-language totality. Compiler proof authority remains false.
#+end_honest

** Model access and recovered values are different facts
Suppose an observation subtracts an unknown integer from itself. The execution form can observe literal zero without choosing that integer. The receiver separately records which original coordinates and observations it could recover.

The native model API can be available, partly available or unavailable. That label belongs to the executed question. Original assertion replay, binding availability and observation availability have their own record. The interface keeps the executed zero alongside the unavailable original coordinate and its incomplete original replay.

Try [[#cell-story-original-replay][that exact case on both engines]]. It keeps a satisfiable question distinct from a completely materialized response point. The response boundary described earlier still checks actual values before publishing a witness.

** Float arithmetic must mean the same thing in the program and the proof
Try adding 0.1 to 1.0 and subtracting 1.0 again. With ideal real numbers, the result is exactly 0.1. In BL's binary64 arithmetic, it is a nearby value. That small difference exposed a large problem: the old process verifier reported a complete proof of a claim that the original compiled predicate made false.

The repair gives runtime Floats their own typed model. The question carries the exact input bits, the rounding mode, and whether the computation succeeds. BL can evaluate those terms independently, then run the original compiled function at the same input and compare both outcomes.

Checkpoint 41 passes 19,415 assertion executions, the compiler artifact floor and source gates. Four Source subjects are compared on both engines; another process reopens eight complete records with zero managed query calls in the named rechecking window. All 187 preceding native statuses and verdicts remain unchanged.

#+begin_rosetta :title "Small distinctions with real consequences"
Program observation | Typed meaning | Why it matters
Adding decimal 0.1 | The Reader's actual binary64 value, then RNE arithmetic | The proof cannot substitute an ideal decimal fraction.
Positive and negative zero | Equal numerically, different returned bits | Numeric equality and strict identity answer different questions.
Negating zero | Flip its sign | Unary negation cannot be replaced with subtraction from zero.
Overflow or division by zero | The operation fails | Failure stays in the claim instead of disappearing into an assumption.
Gradual underflow | A finite subnormal or signed zero succeeds | Tiny results are not all arithmetic failures.
Native SAT without input bits | A candidate with missing evidence | BL never invents a Float coordinate to complete a witness.
Mathematical NaN | One semantic value, no stable payload promise | Independent arithmetic does not manufacture an unavailable native payload.
#+end_rosetta

The arithmetic checks compare 26 selected binary64 input patterns, a separate tiny-format finite grid across five rounding modes, and 160 pinned Z3 reference questions. Tiny-format division uses nonzero divisors; separate special-value checks cover zero division and nonfinite results. This is selected binary64 testing, not exhaustive testing of all binary64 values.

The four new Source subjects make the current backend difference concrete. These are results from the pinned artifacts, not a prediction about future Z3 or OxiZ releases.

#+begin_matrix :title "One binary64 question, different available evidence"
Subject | Z3 | OxiZ | What BL additionally observes
Cancellation by 0.1 | Refuted; actual Source reproduces it | Unknown | The original predicate is false at 1.0.
Negating positive zero | Refuted strict-identity promise | Candidate; original replay incomplete | The returned Float has the negative-zero bits.
Division by zero | Refuted normal-result promise | Candidate; original replay incomplete | The original function raises.
One exact rounded sum | Unchecked solver claim | Unknown | This concrete Source call and independent terms agree.
#+end_matrix

#+begin_honest :title "Exact terms are one part of a formal language"
A richer numeric interface removes a known mismatch and makes more Source claims expressible. It does not make all BL programs total. General recursion, effects, runtime bindings, compiler preservation and arbitrary mixed numeric coercions still need their own contracts and evidence. Native UNSAT remains an unchecked solver claim in this Float fragment.
#+end_honest

#+begin_note :title "Term passing has its own size boundary"
The complete public contents of all 187 earlier questions match on the old and new SDKs. One three-key closure question expands to 110,551 walked data nodes, exceeding the standard identity encoder's unchanged 100,000-node budget. Its native DAG question still runs. The audit uses a separately profiled bounded component hash for inspection; it does not relax runtime limits or create a new proof identity. Sharing large term graphs in portable question identities remains useful work.
#+end_note

** An explicit input can outlive a missing solver model
A backend can struggle with a question even when a useful counterexample is already in your hand. You should be able to give BL that input and ask it to check the original claim directly.

That is now a separate interface. BL first checks a mathematical point against the complete question. It can then run the declared Source function at the corresponding runtime input and compare the outcome. This keeps the native answer, the independent data check, and the actual call as three inspectable facts.

Checkpoint 42 passes 19,726 assertion executions, the compiler artifact floor and source gates. All 187 preceding native statuses and verdicts remain unchanged. Four subjects run on both engines from an unrelated SDK-only directory. Eight saved points and six separately repeated Source observations survive a fresh process.

#+begin_rosetta :title "Three answers that can coexist"
Layer | What it establishes | What it keeps separate
Native backend | Its own answer and available model coordinates | Unknown remains unknown; an explicit point is not a recovered native model.
Independent point checker | The original assertions and counterexample condition hold at supplied typed coordinates | This uses the reviewed data evaluator, not the restricted Int/Bool metatheory.
Actual Source observation | This compiled invocation reproduces the counterexample | Fresh compilation can execute phase code; one call is not universal compiler preservation.
#+end_rosetta

In the paired interval examples, OxiZ returns unknown for cancellation, signed-zero identity and zero division. BL still checks each explicitly supplied point and reproduces the failure in actual Source. Z3's native refutations and OxiZ's unknown answers stay in their original fields.

#+begin_matrix :title "A Float range is finite even when we cannot enumerate it"
Domain | Pattern count | Runtime distinctions | Enumeration

Sampling and shrinking use integer bit keys. This avoids overflowing a Float interpolation and avoids numeric deduplication erasing signed zero. The legacy enumeration sentinel still says =:infinite= for a range above 10,000 values; the new domain description reports /finite, over budget/ explicitly.

Source result equivalence also keeps runtime kind. Returning Int 0 and Float 0.0 differs even though numeric equality agrees. Both sides raising can still be equivalent under the declared coarse raisedness profile; exception categories remain outside that relation.

** A large question should still travel as language data
A formal question contains more than its main formula. Its assumptions, displayed values, semantic profile and provenance all matter. Saving only a formula digest loses part of the question.

The new public-data graph keeps that whole object. Repeated pieces are stored once, and a fresh process can check their structure before rebuilding the values. This closes a concrete gap: the three-key closure question fits the native term DAG but exceeds the old complete-data identity walk.

The complete closure question now uses 332 unique nodes and 10,685 canonical bytes. Its expanded public contents remain 110,551 nodes. The standard encoder still refuses that expansion; the new reference names its own format explicitly.

Checkpoint 43 passes 8,749 targeted assertion executions, all 187 complete-data round trips, the compiler floor and source gates. Both fresh SDK-only readers recheck the saved graph and point without managed solver queries or Source compilation. All 187 preceding paired native statuses and verdicts remain unchanged. The early-boundary hardening preserves all preceding graph references and the exact owned packet bytes.

#+begin_sequence :title "Keep the whole question across a process boundary"
lanes question · BL data | graph · shared nodes | reader · fresh process | point · original assertions
question -> graph : Preserve every public field and share repeated structure.
graph --> question : Return a canonical graph and its versioned reference.
graph -> reader : Save the root and node table for a fresh process.
reader -> reader : Check ordering, references, reachability and expansion limits.
reader => point : Rebuild the original obligations and replay the supplied coordinates.
#+end_sequence

#+begin_rosetta :title "Three different promises"
Operation | What it checks | What may still be refused
Check graph | Canonical closed data and explicit size limits | An invalid or oversized graph
Decode graph | Safe construction of values from that checked data | An unfamiliar keyword or collapsed map/set keys
Check point | All original assertions at explicit typed coordinates | False assertions, missing inputs or unsupported mathematics
#+end_rosetta

#+begin_honest :title "This is a reusable data boundary"
The fresh-process point example belongs to a separate satisfiability question that keeps the whole transitivity question as provenance and displays its nine inputs. It is not a counterexample to transitivity.

The graph does not add a solver theory or prove compiler correctness. It lets BL carry richer questions and independently checked points without discarding their context. Typed answer binding and verdict-ledger migration are the next consumers; the public byte-stream parser and a machine-checked graph model remain separate work.
#+end_honest

Try [[#cell-story-question-graph][a thousand repeated pieces]], [[#cell-story-graph-point][an explicitly versioned point]] and [[#cell-story-graph-budget][a small expansion bomb]]. Read [[file:QUESTION-GRAPHS.md][the format and limits]], [[file:sdk-src/formal/data-graph.bl][the SDK implementation]], [[file:question-graphs-check.bl][the whole-corpus comparison]], [[file:question-graph-consumer.bl][the fresh SDK-only reader]] and [[file:evidence/question-graphs/qualification.json][the exact qualification]].

#+begin_note :title "Preview gives us room to make the rules coherent"
The current SDK keeps Int and Float keys separate, and keeps both zero signs as separate hash keys. Numeric equality has its own behavior. A formal collection API must name its key relation rather than borrow a familiar equality law by accident.

The audit also exposed a sequencing assumption in its own report: a map literal sampled a counter before the walk that filled it. The report now uses an explicit =let=. Read [[file:SEMANTIC-REVIEW-KEYS.md][the equality and literal-order review]] for the language choices still to settle.
#+end_note

** Use the saved question without unpacking its history
A saved question should be useful to the next program. It should be possible to send it to either solver, inspect an answer, or keep a durable record without first rebuilding every piece of its history.

The common BL interface now carries a graph-backed question. Each consumer checks the complete saved graph and rebuilds the typed terms it actually needs. Unfamiliar provenance can stay ordinary graph data. Changing that provenance still changes the complete question reference.

The first corpus run hit its overall 15-minute ceiling; the completed timed rerun retains the same solver deadlines. These are interface and instance checks, not a speed comparison or a compiler proof.
#+end_note

The compiler artifact is different from checkpoint 43: it adopts main's existing eager =doseq= fix. Its complete floor and 25 focused checks now pass, including effects under a zero memo budget. The formal worker artifact is unchanged. This qualification does not establish compiler soundness.

The separate [[file:GRAPH-TABLE-MODEL.md][finite graph-table model]] now passes Lean: 15 general results, six original-value instance theorems and 16 rejection controls. The original closure graph has 332 stored nodes, 110,551 expanded nodes and depth 26 in both the SDK report and the checked model. This is a proof of structural quantities, not transitivity. Scalar byte costs, runtime admission and the BL implementation remain separate connections.

The new [[file:GRAPH-SELECTION-MODEL.md][selection model]] answers a different question: if we read only the needed part, do we get its original value? Lean now checks seven general lemmas, five fixture theorems and four rejection controls. In the model, reading the integer seven can succeed while unrelated unfamiliar history remains unadmitted. Reading the complete value can still refuse.

#+begin_rosetta :title "What the selection proof requires"
I select… | What the model establishes | What still needs a connection
A closed set of correctly admitted nodes | Each selected value agrees with its complete original value. | Prove BL's actual dependency selector builds that closed set.
Only a container, leaving out its children | The readiness premise cannot be supplied. | A selected parent needs its dependencies.
A value containing an unadmitted keyword | Full materialization refuses in the fixture. | Runtime keyword and collection admission remain explicit BL policies.
#+end_rosetta

Read [[file:metatheory/GraphSelection.lean][the general proof]], [[file:metatheory/GraphSelectionExamples.lean][the concrete fixture]] and [[file:evidence/graph-selection-model/qualification.json][the actual model qualification]]. These mathematical results do not yet prove the implementation's reachability loop, typed-term decoder or native translation.

Three boundaries stay visible. An engine's UNSAT answer is still an unchecked solver claim. Reopening a saved execution label does not establish a new execution. A graph that preserves the full question does not by itself prove the evaluator, compiler, or whole language total.

Try [[#cell-story-graph-selected][the selected view]], [[#cell-story-graph-native][both native replies]], [[#cell-story-graph-intercepted][an intercepted answer]] and [[#cell-story-graph-durable][the reopened record]]. The four cells keep the same complete question reference.

Read [[file:GRAPH-CONSUMERS.md][the interface and limits]], [[file:sdk-src/formal/question-graph.bl][the authoritative question wrapper]], [[file:graph-consumers-check.bl][the paired corpus recipe]] and [[file:graph-ledger-consumer.bl][the fresh reader recipe]]. The [[file:evidence/graph-consumers/qualification.json][complete code qualification]] retains the actual artifacts and limits. The separate [[file:evidence/graph-consumers/dossier-qualification.json][presentation receipt]] records executable cells, the live host and actual browser frames.

** A small graph can reach either engine without growing into a tree
A term can point to the same earlier piece twice. Repeating that pattern grows the expanded expression quickly, while the saved graph remains small. The new BL project prototype keeps those references through typed preparation and into the native protocol both engines already accept.

The live example below builds 45 stored data nodes describing 131,071 term occurrences. It prepares 17 native definitions and checks 17 scope states. Z3 and OxiZ each receive a 772-byte request and return the observed true root.

#+begin_stats
521 | focused assertions | Primitive families, canonical fields, original export agreement and binder-environment controls.
187 | exact preparation matches | Every preceding question keeps the same native definitions and ordered roots.
17 | native term definitions | The graph-built fixture describes 131,071 term occurrences.
772 B | request to either engine | Two actual raw scopes return the same observed true root.
#+end_stats

#+begin_sequence :title "Keep the shared pieces, then make the native call"
lanes graph · 45 stored data nodes | typed · 17 checked definitions | owner · fresh raw context | engine · Z3 or OxiZ
graph -> typed : Follow argument references; check local sorts and each binder environment.
typed -> owner : Supply a fresh table and its translated root ID.
owner => engine : Send the 772-byte request under the existing deadlines.
engine --> owner : SAT and an observed true root.
owner --> graph : Record the raw result; invalidate managed portable-context accounting.
#+end_sequence

#+begin_note :title "A qualified project preview"
The forest remains a project module on the pinned SDK. The default graph-query interface retains its qualified operational view. Project previews now also cover complete-question inventory, structured observations and bounded point evaluation; actual managed-owner admission and full assertion replay remain next. A prepared table is neither a proof object nor an accepted cached question view.
#+end_note

The wider raw protocol run samples 19 theory families on each engine. These are native statuses; UNSAT is still an unchecked engine claim, and unknown or unsupported is not a theorem.

#+begin_rosetta :title "The same prepared interface keeps different capabilities visible"
Engine | Observed native outcomes across 19 families | What is retained
Z3 | 15 UNSAT · 3 SAT · 1 unsupported | Actual SDK worker/library identity and raw reply.
OxiZ | 11 UNSAT · 3 SAT · 2 unknown · 3 unsupported | Existing capability gaps stay explicit.
Both | The sharing fixture returns the observed true root. | Raw success invalidates managed portable-context accounting.
#+end_rosetta

There is no blanket speed claim. Across the 187 preparation cases, the new path totals 15.45 seconds versus 4.70 seconds for the existing exporter, with initial graph conversion recorded separately. Small inputs pay more graph-checking overhead. The shared closure case prepares in 870 ms versus 2,055 ms after an 8.19-second initial conversion. These are preparation observations, not end-to-end query or solver benchmarks.

Try [[#cell-story-term-forest][the actual shared native call]]. Read [[file:TERM-FOREST.md][the capability and limits]], [[file:TERM-FOREST-PLAN.md][the architecture and semantic choices]], [[file:src/formal/term-forest.bl][the preparer]] and [[file:evidence/term-forest/qualification.json][the code qualification]]. Its earlier [[file:evidence/term-forest/dossier-qualification.json][81-cell presentation receipt]] remains available.

** BL can evaluate the shared pieces too
Preparing a small native packet is only half the story. BL must also check candidate values independently. The new project evaluator follows the saved argument references, remembers each demanded result, and preserves the current SDK's evaluation rules.

The same 131,071-occurrence fixture now evaluates through 17 typed nodes. Asking for its root twice returns =[true true]= without calling either engine. Across all 187 earlier questions, four point maps per question produce 6,300 exact matches with the existing evaluator, including 1,484 incomplete results.

#+begin_stats
92 | focused assertions | Demand order, incomplete reasons, forged input and shared evaluation.
748 | point maps compared | Missing, zero, alternate and ill-sorted coordinates on the existing questions.
6,300 | exact root comparisons | Incomplete mathematics stays incomplete.
17 | evaluated nodes | Two requested roots share the same computed result.
#+end_stats

#+begin_rosetta :title "Keep the evaluation rules visible"
Expression | What is demanded | Why it matters
If | The condition, then its chosen branch. | An unused result need not be computed.
And / or | Children until a decisive Boolean appears. | Missing coordinates do not force every later branch.
Implication | Both operands, as in the current SDK. | A new representation must not quietly change language meaning.
All admitted terms | Local typing and binder checks still run. | An unused branch cannot hide malformed code.
#+end_rosetta

This is still a project preview, outside managed query/reply and ledger checking. Small cases cost more: the serial run took 66.5 seconds including fresh graph admission/preparation, versus 0.41 seconds on already materialized terms. These are different preparation costs, not a solver or end-to-end benchmark.

Try [[#cell-story-graph-point-2][the actual BL evaluation]]. Read [[file:TERM-FOREST-EVALUATION.md][the interface and limits]], [[file:src/formal/term-forest-evaluate.bl][the graph evaluator]], [[file:evidence/term-forest-evaluation/qualification.json][the code qualification]] and [[file:evidence/term-forest-evaluation/dossier-qualification.json][the separate presentation receipt]]. Quantified interpretation and a mechanical proof connecting the two evaluators remain open.

*** Try changing how many layers share their children
Each new layer points twice to the previous result. The tree description repeats that work; the typed graph adds one node. Move the control and click a number to inspect its formula. This calculator counts syntax; it does not run the solver or record your adjustments as evidence.

#+begin_measure
value sharing_terms_16 | Term occurrences in the expanded expression | count | gold | Each active layer has two children.
value sharing_nodes | Typed graph nodes | count | green | One leaf plus one node for each active layer.
#+end_measure

#+begin_controls :inputs "sharing_layers" :title "Change the number of shared layers"
#+end_controls

bar sharing_terms_16 | Expanded term occurrences | A count predicted by the illustrative structural model. | gold
bar sharing_nodes | Typed graph nodes | At 16 layers, the actual checked example evaluates 17 nodes. | green
#+end_bars

The default of 16 layers matches the actual checked fixture. The other settings are illustrative counts checked against the same counting formula, not additional executions of the evaluator. Formula inspection and controls run locally in the browser.

** Keep the question attached to its shared terms
A formula is only part of a question. The assumptions, the claim, what would count as a counterexample, and the values we want to inspect must travel together. The new project preparer now reads that inventory straight from the complete original graph.

All 187 earlier questions keep exactly the same native definitions and root order. Their assertion labels, claim/counterexample IDs, observation term leaves, proof/SAT mode and vacuity policy match too. The current 71 focused assertions also check forgeries and aliases. No engine or managed query is called by preparation.

#+begin_rosetta :title "What the inventory keeps together"
Part of the question | What is checked or retained | What it means
Assertions and claim | Boolean roots and their original labels. | Assumptions and the property retain their roles.
Counterexample | Fresh local typing and syntax agreement with the negated claim. | Matching a node ID alone would miss accepted list/vector aliases.
Observed values | The same ordered term leaves, beside their original descriptor graph references. | This inventory is not yet a reconstructed model witness.
Mode and vacuity | Proof/SAT distinction and the existing feasibility policy. | A SAT question cannot quietly acquire a proof claim.
Uninterpreted data | The complete original graph reference. | Unread metadata and observation leaves remain bound data.
#+end_rosetta

#+begin_note :title "Two admission details repaired centrally"
The preceding SDK's query validator can compare a forged Int datum =1.0= as equal to Int =1= inside the negated claim, because its typed-root walk omits the counterexample. Its actual exporter and the new preparer refuse that input. Observation collection also visits lists while that decoder reconstructs maps/vectors. The qualified observation SDK repairs both common interfaces. These findings do not show native execution accepting an ill-typed query.
#+end_note

One new capability needs an explicit boundary: the inventory can keep an unfamiliar observation keyword as original graph data without materializing it. It returns no operational view or decoded witness for that leaf. The separate observation interpreter now gives a demanded leaf an explicit materialization refusal. A complete observation-domain grammar remains open.

Read [[file:QUESTION-FOREST.md][the interface and limits]], [[file:src/formal/question-forest.bl][the graph preparer]], [[file:evidence/question-forest/qualification.json][the code qualification]] and [[file:QUESTION-FOREST-PLAN.md][the architecture and semantic review]]. Before managed promotion, connect structured observations and owner/revision checks, and add [[file:TERM-FOREST-RESOURCE-PLAN.md][budgets for derived values]]. A finite term graph can still describe a very large concrete integer or collection.

The [[file:evidence/question-forest/dossier-qualification.json][separate presentation receipt]] retains the unchanged executable cells and the new browser review. No complete question-interpretation theorem or compiler proof is claimed.

** Let observations demand only what they display
A saved question may describe several possible outcomes. Reading its useful values should preserve lists, nil and false, and should not unpack a payload that the chosen outcome never uses.

The new project interpreter now reconstructs structured observations from the original question graph. It shares one point evaluator across demanded roots, and uses a scoped data reader for the metadata it actually needs. For all 187 earlier questions, four deterministic point maps produce 1,176 exact observation matches and 6,300 exact root-value matches with the candidate SDK.

#+begin_sequence :title "The same question, two different demands"
lanes question · original shared data | point · supplied assignments | display · reconstructed observation
question -> point : Keep the option's presence condition and original payload reference.
point -> display : Presence is false: show the absent option without reading its item.
point -> display : Presence is true: request the item and report any materialization refusal.
note question, display : Both results remain attached to the same original question. Neither grants a solver verdict.
#+end_sequence

#+begin_rosetta :title "The value tells you what happened"
Situation | Display result | Meaning
List payload | A list containing its reconstructed values, nil and false. | Ordered values keep their container kind.
Inactive option item | An absent option; no payload refusal. | The item was not demanded.
Active unfamiliar payload | An explicit observation-materialization refusal and its descriptor graph. | The payload stays bound data; it did not become a concrete value.
Missing mathematical coordinate | The existing incomplete-value result. | Display reconstruction has no value to invent.
#+end_rosetta

The common SDK changes also validate the actual counterexample's local types and require each top-level observer to be a typed term or explicit semantic value. =v/make= is the explicit wrapper for a composite payload. List decoding and list branch selection now preserve lists. The focused runs pass 62 common semantic assertions, 16 actual paired native list assertions and 298 project graph assertions.

#+begin_honest :title "Code qualified; presentation checked separately"
The new project pin selects the qualified immutable SDK. It passes 9,964 targeted assertions, all 187 exact question inventories and all 187 preceding paired native outcomes. Two fresh SDK-only readers recheck the saved graph evidence without solver calls. Observation reconstruction makes no managed queries. That corpus took about 14 minutes; no speed advantage is established. The dossier separates 85 live cells from one explicitly recorded native observation. The earlier presentation receipt covers its recorded source version; browser behavior remains a separate gate.
#+end_honest

The next gate is concrete-value growth. Seventeen shared nodes can repeatedly square an integer until the result is much larger than the graph. Add preflight limits for derived values before connecting this interpreter to managed assertion replay, retained assumptions and owner revisions. Reconstruction currently reports =assertion-replay :not-run=.

The first [[file:INTEGER-PREFLIGHT.md][integer resource planner]] passes [[file:evidence/observation-semantics/integer-preflight-qualification.json][10,248 focused assertions]]. It returns ordinary BL terms describing input widths, result and temporary-size forecasts, limits and any incomplete reason. It does not run the arithmetic primitive. The new bounded scalar evaluator now uses its forecasts before integer execution. The [[file:value-growth-probe.bl][three capped growth probes]] compute a 65,537-bit integer and a 65,536-byte string through 17 typed nodes each, plus a 4,096-element sequence through 14 nodes. Limits govern whether checking can finish; they do not turn mathematical Int into bounded arithmetic.

Code compatibility, cell execution and browser behavior remain different kinds of evidence.

** Give checking a budget without changing mathematical integers
A short expression can ask for a huge number. Sharing its syntax saves space, but the number still has to fit somewhere. BL now has a separate Bool/Int evaluation profile that checks whether the next integer operation fits /before/ running it.

In the [[#cell-story-bounded-point][running example]], 17 shared term nodes describe 16 successive squarings. With a 32-bit value allowance, the evaluator performs four multiplications, charges 119 integer work units, then refuses the next operation's 34-bit forecast. Asking for the same root twice spends that shared work once.

#+begin_sequence :title "Reserve before computing"
lanes graph · original term graph | budget · private request accounting | sdk · existing integer semantics
graph -> budget : Demand a node and obtain its actual child values.
budget -> budget : Forecast integer size, temporary size and work; check the remaining allowance.
budget -> sdk : Fits: reserve the work, then run the existing primitive.
sdk -> graph : Memoize the concrete value for every later request for that node.
budget -> graph : Does not fit: return an explicit incomplete value and retain the reason.
note graph, sdk : No solver is called. The integer's mathematical meaning does not change.
#+end_sequence

#+begin_rosetta :title "A refusal does not erase what we can still know"
Question | Result | What BL keeps
Seven times nine, with enough room | 63 | The existing SDK's arithmetic meaning.
The shared squaring graph, under the small allowance | Incomplete | The refused forecast and work already performed.
A refused predicate followed by false in AND | False; root complete | The earlier refusal remains visible in diagnostics.
A cheaper later root after expensive work was refused | Can still complete | Work that never ran did not spend its allowance.
#+end_rosetta

The result binds the original graph, supplied assignments and normalized settings separately. The ordered roots also belong to its identity: a cumulative budget can make order matter. Only the owning BEAM process can use the scoped evaluator or data reader, and their callbacks retire when the operation ends.

#+begin_honest :title "A precise first resource profile"
The six focused files pass 1,170 assertions, including the earlier graph consumers. This profile admits Bool and Int term nodes. Other sorts refuse admission; operations without a cost policy remain explicitly incomplete. Arithmetic work units are a declared bit-size convention, not a theorem about CPU time or heap use. A separate numeric profile now adds exact fractions. Text, sequences, arrays, floating point and actual managed assertion replay remain next. Compiler proof authority stays false.
#+end_honest

The [[file:evidence/bounded-point/compatibility-qualification.json][separate general-profile comparison]] also passes: all 187 questions, 748 point maps and 6,300 root values agree exactly with the selected SDK. Their preceding value fingerprints and evaluation counters remain identical. This covers the shared traversal across existing theories; those theories still need their own resource policies.

Read [[file:BOUNDED-POINT.md][the interface and accounting rules]], [[file:src/formal/term-forest-evaluate.bl][the evaluator]], [[file:src/formal/bounded-point-examples.bl][the actual example]], [[file:test/bounded_point_test.bl][the semantic and resource checks]] and [[file:evidence/bounded-point/qualification.json][the code qualification]]. Cell and browser checks have a [[file:evidence/bounded-point/dossier-qualification.json][separate presentation receipt]]. The [[file:TERM-FOREST-RESOURCE-PLAN.md][wider resource gate]] remains open.

** Checking a fraction starts before the arithmetic
Exact fractions belong in the same term language as integers. The new numeric profile evaluates =1/3 + 1/6= as =1/2=, without calling a solver. It also accounts for a less obvious cost: checking that a fraction is in its canonical form already needs normalization.

BL now separates two allowances. /Admission/ checks the expression itself, including literals in unused branches. /Evaluation/ follows the pieces that the current point actually needs. If admission cannot finish, no partially checked expression is allowed to run. Once admission succeeds, a refused calculation can coexist with a decisive Boolean answer.

#+begin_sequence :title "Two phases, two honest ways to stop"
lanes graph · original term graph | admission · check the expression | evaluation · follow demand
graph -> admission : Reserve all fraction-literal normalization before any SDK constructor runs.
admission -> graph : Cannot fit: return admission incomplete; no normalizer has run.
admission -> evaluation : Fits: check canonical syntax, then start with a separate evaluation allowance.
evaluation -> evaluation : Reserve each demanded operation before cross-products and normalization.
evaluation -> graph : Return concrete values or explicit evaluation incompleteness, with both accounts attached.
#+end_sequence

The [[#cell-story-numeric-point][executed example]] repeatedly squares =3/2= through 17 shared term nodes. A 32-bit component allowance admits four multiplications, then refuses the next 52-bit forecast. The same saved graph under a zero admission allowance stops earlier: no SDK normalization or evaluation runs. Its original graph identity stays the same; its settings identity changes.

#+begin_rosetta :title "Keep these outcomes separate"
Situation | Outcome | Meaning
Literal normalization cannot fit | Admission incomplete | No typed forest or partially checked expression is granted.
An admitted operation cannot fit | Evaluation incomplete | Its cross-products never run; earlier work stays recorded.
A false AND branch skips fractions | False, after syntax admission | Literals were checked; no concrete fraction arithmetic was demanded.
A refused predicate is followed by false | False; root complete | The earlier evaluation refusal remains visible.
#+end_rosetta

This distinction is checked with actual BEAM function-call traces, including positive controls. Denied admission makes zero calls to the SDK normalizer. Denied addition and multiplication make zero calls to their arithmetic functions. The full run passes 4,412 assertion executions across nine files. All 187 general-profile questions still retain 6,300 exact values at 748 deterministic point maps.

#+begin_honest :title "The traces show the next simplification too"
The current graph adapter still rechecks some already admitted fraction values through shallow SDK bindings. In the small multiplication control, the graph path calls the normalizer seven times; direct SDK evaluation calls it three times. Both produce the same value. This extra work is budgeted, and removing the repeated checks is a concrete job for the shared SDK primitive interface. No speed advantage, totality theorem or compiler proof is claimed.
#+end_honest

Read [[file:NUMERIC-POINT.md][the numeric interface and phase rules]], [[file:src/formal/rational-preflight.bl][the fraction planner]], [[file:test/numeric_execution_trace_test.bl][the actual call controls]], [[file:evidence/numeric-point/trace.json][the recorded calls]], [[file:evidence/numeric-point/qualification.json][the code and corpus qualification]] and [[file:evidence/numeric-point/dossier-qualification.json][the separate presentation receipt]]. Native calls stay zero and assertion replay remains not run in this point preview.

The [[file:src/formal/numeric-point-examples.bl][example source]] and [[file:test/numeric_point_test.bl][numeric semantic controls]] keep the displayed results connected to their original terms.

** One SDK function for tree and graph values
The same multiplication should mean the same thing whether it arrives as a small tree or a shared graph. Both evaluators now call one private SDK implementation. The graph path freshly admits the original terms, then reuses its own checked child values. A caller cannot supply a checked marker to skip that work.

For =1/3 × 2/5=, both paths return =2/15=. The earlier numeric graph policy made seven normalizer calls: it checked some fractions again while passing child values through shallow terms. Numeric v2 makes three calls, matching the ordinary evaluator. These are [[file:evidence/shared-primitives/sdk-traces.json][actual SDK call controls]], not a timing benchmark.

#+begin_rosetta :title "Same fraction, explicit execution policy"
Path | Normalizer calls | Evaluation work | Literal admission
Ordinary tree evaluator | 3 | No graph work account | Not a graph operation
Numeric graph v1 | 7 | 1,600 units | 364 units
Numeric graph v2 | 3 | 872 units | 364 units
#+end_rosetta

The [[#cell-story-shared-primitives][executed comparison]] gives both graph policies an evaluation allowance of 872 units. V2 completes; v1 reports incomplete. The original graph stays identical. The settings and execution references differ because the policy changed. Existing project APIs explicitly keep their earlier profiles; new callers select v2 through the common SDK interface.

#+begin_sequence :title "Original terms reach shared mathematics"
lanes caller · portable terms | admission · SDK scope | mathematics · private primitives | result · portable values
caller -> admission : Supply the original graph, root IDs, assignments and settings.
admission -> admission : Validate syntax and scope; reserve literal normalization.
admission -> mathematics : Demand a node; forecast work from private checked children.
mathematics --> admission : Return the value under the same rules as tree evaluation.
admission => result : Keep values, completion, refusals and execution references separate.
note admission, result : Local callbacks retire; no native handle becomes query data.
#+end_sequence

The [[file:evidence/shared-primitives/qualification.json][qualified immutable SDK]] passes 14,167 targeted assertion executions, all 187 point questions at four point maps each, and two fresh SDK-only readers. All 187 paired native questions retain their complete identities and preceding outcomes at unchanged solver deadlines. That checkpoint selected its exact SDK; its adapter and dossier receipts remain historical. The current project selection is recorded below.

#+begin_honest :title "One implementation still needs its own proof connection"
BL privacy does not hide the native BEAM bridge. Qualified BL access to the private helper is refused, while raw native interop remains a different boundary. No speed advantage, universal cost theorem, totality or compiler preservation is established here. The owner transaction below now adds original-assertion replay alongside the same private mathematics and keeps display observations separate.
#+end_honest

Read the [[file:SHARED-PRIMITIVES.md][SDK interface and privacy rules]], [[file:MANAGED-GRAPH-PLAN.md][owner-side transaction design]], [[file:evidence/shared-primitives/qualification-progress.json][integration progress]] and [[file:evidence/shared-primitives/sdk-candidate.json][exact SDK pin]].

The project delegates are directly inspectable: [[file:src/formal/integer-preflight.bl][integer policy]], [[file:src/formal/rational-preflight.bl][rational policy]], [[file:src/formal/term-forest.bl][typed admission]] and [[file:src/formal/term-forest-evaluate.bl][point evaluation]]. The [[file:evidence/shared-primitives/integration-qualification.json][separate integration receipt]] binds those files and their 4,412 passing assertions to the promoted SDK.

** A byte can carry its meaning across both solvers
A counter that fits in eight bits wraps from =255= to =0=. That is a different promise from unbounded integer addition. Beam Lisp can now keep the width, the operations, the remembered facts and the checking rules together in one portable question.

The [[#cell-story-word-request][executed byte example]] asks both Z3 and OxiZ about that wrap. BL then checks the returned point using its own word operations. Change the saved counter to =0= and the same reader rejects it: the solver's status cannot make a wrong point true.

#+begin_rosetta :title "One request, several ways to answer"
Route | What crosses the boundary | What BL checks
Existing native owner | Complete graph question and explicit word policy | Every retained fact and demanded word operation.
Fresh native owner | The same portable request | The same point relation under the same policy.
Supplied point handler | An eight-bit coordinate, as ordinary BL data | Width, value range, resources and the actual proposition.
Saved record reader | A graph packet or a ledger ID | Fresh point replay, without asking a solver.
#+end_rosetta

#+begin_graph :title "Width belongs to the question"
node terms | Word terms | Width and operations | 0,0 | you
node policy | Checking policy | Grammar and budgets | 1,1 | station
node request | Complete request | Terms and assumptions | 1,0 | door
node engines | Z3 or OxiZ | Candidate coordinates | 2,0 | builder
node replay | Fresh BL replay | Check the actual point | 3,0 | you
edge terms -> request | describe | solid
edge policy -> request | bound | solid
edge request -> engines | ask | solid
edge engines -> replay | inspect | solid
#+end_graph

The resource rules apply before expensive word operations. Admission checks even an unused declaration's width and reserves literal work before constructing terms. Point replay charges the coordinates it actually needs and the operations it demands. Raising a work allowance cannot enlarge the language's fixed width grammar.

#+begin_sequence :title "Check the budget before making the value"
lanes graph · original question | admission · BL policy | owner · chosen engine | reader · fresh replay
graph -> admission : Inspect sorts and reserve all literal work.
admission -> owner : Only an admitted complete question reaches native execution.
owner --> reader : Return a status and any word coordinates.
reader -> reader : Reserve demanded work before checking values and operations.
note admission, reader : A budget refusal is incomplete checking. It is not a false mathematical claim.
#+end_sequence

Saved answers also have a more direct storage path. Opening one ledger ID validates that row's existing payload and freshly checks its point. The qualification measures one private row-normalizer call per selected lookup; unrelated records are not decoded on that path. This is an access-path measurement, not a general latency benchmark.

The saved comparison below reads the actual qualification records. Its sources, scope and basis stay with the view. A later source change marks the saved view stale until its owner explicitly refreshes it.

#+begin_research_element :id word-answer-evidence
Saved evidence comparison · qualified word SDK · native Org snapshot.
#+end_research_element

#+begin_honest :title "What this adds to a formal language"
This policy gives typed word propositions and independently replayed finite points a common home in BL. A native unsatisfiable answer remains a solver claim. Quantifier replay, further word operators, a checked word proof format, Source-level word outcomes and a compiler preservation theorem need their own implementations and evidence. The separate Lean magnitude model proves arithmetic bounds; it does not prove this evaluator or either solver correct.
#+end_honest

** Small proofs and careful text interfaces
Three further prototypes now run against the same qualified word SDK. They show how a common formal interface can grow without asking you to treat an engine's confidence as a proof.

#+begin_rosetta :title "What the next layer can already check"
Prototype | Actual result | What remains separate
Finite word certificates | 82 assertions; the wrapping theorem checked over all eight three-bit values | The exact assumptions remain visible; their feasibility and compiler correspondence are unestablished.
Scalar text terms and budgets | 512 assertions; total, Option and strict access use explicit codepoint positions | Ordinary BL String operations keep their existing meaning; managed text integration is still a candidate.
Evidence bundles | 47 assertions across five named tests; a fresh reader reopened two packets with six refusal controls and zero solver calls | Native status, point validity, certificate checking and execution history remain separate facts.
#+end_rosetta

A checked finite certificate can keep the same mathematical statement when you switch the requested engine. The outer evidence bundle still binds your complete request. Reopening a saved packet runs the checks again using the reader's current proof allowance. A producer's saved success is never accepted as the answer.

Text makes the semantic choice concrete. In =é=, the base letter and combining accent occupy two codepoint positions even though BL's grapheme length is one. The prototype offers an explicit Option accessor and a deliberately strict formal accessor alongside the total SMT operation. It does not quietly reinterpret an ordinary BL string program.

The same symbolic combining-character contract produced =UNSAT= in Z3 and =UNKNOWN= in OxiZ. Concrete BL replay evaluated it to true. That is a capability observation about this question and these pinned engines; the shared term interface preserves the difference instead of inventing an OxiZ result.

#+begin_note :title "The runtime candidate has passed its native audit"
The candidate SDK is built and has passed 11,705 assertions. All 187 paired Z3/OxiZ questions retain their preceding results and solver binaries. Fresh SDK-only readers have reopened twelve older word packets, four text packets and two certificate bundles; the text gate passed 52 checks and the bundle gate ran six fresh finite checks, with no solver or oracle calls.

Twenty new word/text records and an actual durable ledger are now being captured. Two fresh durable readers and older-generation compatibility checks still have to pass before selection. The executable examples and saved comparison on this page retain the qualified word SDK checkpoint; the new runtime is not selected yet.

The pending [[file:FINITE-FEASIBILITY-PROTOTYPE.md][feasibility]] and [[file:FINITE-OBLIGATION-PROTOTYPE.md][policy composition]] experiments have not run.
